# Phase 5 history

Phase 5 closed on 2026-11-28 (owner). The first sections were moved here on 2026-11-15, while it ran: answered owner questions, the completed-work log, and the phase-5 task table with its full status notes as they stood then. §Phase 5 at closing holds the rest: the final task table, execution order and exit criteria, the last answered questions and closed security findings, and its feature rows.

Moved **verbatim** from `docs/PROGRESS.md` and `docs/FEATURES.md` on 2026-11-15, when PROGRESS was cut down to the current and next phase (owner request). Nothing was reworded; only section headings were added or levelled. References such as "§Phase 4 tasks", "§Summary by phase" or "the next-free note" point at PROGRESS.md as it was then (`git show a7998616:docs/PROGRESS.md`). This file is frozen: corrections go in PROGRESS.md, FEATURES.md or a new decision, not here.

## Answered owner questions (from PROGRESS §Decisions requiring user input)

- **Answered 2026-11-14: FIX-142 Mattermost questions → D-270, D-271** — the step-4 link-preview question: bare URLs and autolinks in an agent's message become `[url](url)` (the server prefetches them with no click; a fifth D-227 exception); every Mattermost ping accounted for (`@channel`/`@all` in every spelling never notify, `@here` only via D-226(b), group mentions defused and fail closed, `@username` passes, nothing in code notifies). FIX-142 implements both on `p5-platform`.
- **Answered 2026-11-14: P5-CHAT-0 boards approved** (cdea8832) — "all approved", incl. the designer's open choices (Copy link in More, two-row phone composer, 320 px search pane, local-day date filters, drafts keyed by user id and cleared on sign-out); P5-CHAT-2/3 and P5-CHAT-4 B unblocked.
- **Answered 2026-11-14: P4-011B spec questions → D-269** — parking is an instance-wide switch only; with parking on every rule may exceed the turn ceiling; `push.*` stays in Helm; Web Push tested with the mock push service and a service-worker browser test, real delivery a manual release check.
- **Answered 2026-11-14: SSH and LLM-provider spec questions → D-268** — SSH keeps a sandbox awake only while bytes flow (4 h cap), no port forwarding; Azure Entra and Bedrock assume-role in scope now; the `anthropic` path set on by default; coordinator defaults for the minor questions recorded in D-268 for review.
- **Answered 2026-11-14: SPEC-SETTINGS classification and phase-5 spec order → D-235** — (a) sandbox sizes and `max_sandboxes` stay in Helm; (b) OIDC providers editable in the app (Helm providers locked, `auth.instance_admins` stays in Helm); (c) spec order P4-LLM → P4-SSH → P4-011B, then the rest; (d) P5-CERN-ADDONS reaches LDAP through a gateway lane.

- 2026-11-14 (owner, coordinator on master): the shell's `<main>` lost its `max-w-page` cap (pages set their own width, so the agent page now gets the full content width, FIX-129); the terminal's bash/zsh picker (FIX-138, D-246) moved from the Terminal page to a Terminal card on the Computer page (briefly on Instructions first), same per-browser storage (`docs/14` amended). Owner terminal bugs, 2026-11-14/15: (1) the pane is pinned to a full-screen app's (alternate) screen and gives it the wheel (6906cefe on master, `terminal-alt-screen.test.tsx`); (2) `claude`/`codex` in a fresh boot's terminal went to `api.anthropic.com` (401: the agent's key is for another endpoint) because `harness.env` only learned the model at a turn. The terminal open now carries each enabled harness's model (D-247 (e), C-750 (4): API `model::select` at redemption → `pty.open.models` → `harness.env` before the shell starts; wrappers say why when no key/model). Branch `alexiri/terminal-models` (worktree `terminal-models`), targeted gates green (fmt, clippy -D warnings on protocol/supervisor/runner/api, protocol + supervisor tests, runner lib, API `redemption_answer`); not yet on hosted e2e.

Phase-5 briefs written the same day: FIX-141 (Indico find-and-book), FIX-142 (agent Markdown on chat platforms; D-226, D-227), P5-CHAT-0..4 (chat as a messaging client and message search; D-228..D-230, C-725..C-728), FIX-144/FIX-145 (self-hosted apps under a path; D-231, C-730/C-731), FIX-146 (request tracing; D-232, C-732, SEC-88), FIX-147 (one permissions editor; D-233), P5-ADMDEL (D-234, C-733); then the coordinator's spec batches SPEC-SETTINGS, P4-SSH, P4-LLM, P4-011B, P5-CHAT-0 and the step 0 of FIX-142/145/146, and the owner's answers D-235, D-268..D-271.

| FIX-053 | The terminal Helm switch must be set twice: `terminal.enabled=true` alone failed the mirror guard (`terminal.enabled and config.terminal.enabled must agree`) because the guard demanded both keys — the D-159 class (a mirror you must set twice is not a mirror). Fix: top-level `terminal.enabled` is the single switch; `config.terminal.enabled` derives from it when unset; guard fails only on real disagreement (both set unequal → mirror message; config-only true → actionable set-the-top-level message, since only the top-level renders the :4108 listener + NetworkPolicy wiring). Six-case helm template matrix asserted; both-false renders `[terminal] enabled = false` where master omitted the table (app-equivalent; config checksum changes once → pods roll on the next upgrade); relay/previews pair intentionally untouched at FIX-053 time (agreement semantics, not a mirror — owner chose Option B same day → FIX-056) | owner report 2026-11-12 (mid-upgrade blocker) | S | Helm + docs | — | `deploy/helm/aurora/templates/configmap.yaml`, `values.yaml`, docs/23 rows, docs/15 §Upgrades | — | done (a6cad6e5, merged 3e58fcc5): helm lint + guards + relay tests green; owner unblocked with the both-keys workaround pre-merge |
| FIX-054 | The terminal connects but typing is dead (owner production incident 2026-11-12: DevTools showed one WebSocket Text message per keypress with zero echo; the shell printed `cannot set terminal process group (-1)` / `no job control`). Two root causes: (a) web — wterm's `onData` yields a string, `WebSocket.send(string)` emits a Text frame, but the runner's terminal leg reads Binary as PTY bytes and reserves Text for `pty.resize` only, so every keystroke was silently dropped (the ITs drove Binary directly, which is why they passed while the browser path was broken); (b) supervisor — the shell got the PTY slave as stdio but never a controlling terminal (no setsid/TIOCSCTTY), so `tcsetpgrp` fails with ENOTTY and keyboard signals can never work. Fix: (a) the tab UTF-8-encodes keystrokes and sends Binary frames chunked to the 16 KiB `TERMINAL_CHUNK` (resize stays the one Text control), seam-tested against a stub socket incl. paste chunking; (b) a safe self re-exec bootstrap (`__pty-exec`: rustix setsid → ioctl_tiocsctty on fd 0 → exec via std's safe `CommandExt::exec`, since rustix 1.1.6's execvp is a panic stub and exposes no safe setuid) gives the shell a real session + ctty; `forbid(unsafe_code)` intact | owner report 2026-11-12 | M | Rust + web | P4-TERM, FIX-051 | `web/src/components/agents/terminal-tab.tsx` (+ input-seam test), `services/aurora-supervisor/src/pty.rs`, `pty_exec.rs` (new), `spawn.rs`, `main.rs`, IT `pty_env_probe.rs` (proves /dev/pts, clean startup, `kill -0 $$`, monitor mode), docs/14+16 | — | done (aeb64fb8, merged bb8beb73): supervisor 72+72, terminal_streams 4/4, web vitest 545/545, clippy/fmt/build/guards green on the merged tree |
| FIX-056 | The previews Helm switch must be set twice: the relay/previews guard (`configmap.yaml:54`) failed on `relay.enabled=true` alone — same D-159 class as FIX-053, owner chose Option B (FIX-053 semantics here too). Top-level `relay.enabled` is the single switch: `config.previews.enabled` derives when unset; guard fails only on both-set-unequal (mirror message) or config-only-true (actionable — only the top-level renders the relay Deployment, wildcard edge rules and NetworkPolicy wiring); derivation precedes the shared stream-listener line, terminal-on + previews-off regression asserted. | FIX-049 | Sandbox starts fail `home_volume_unbound` on a transient PVC provisioning race (owner's production cluster, 2026-11-12: `ProvisioningFailed: Error saving claim … object has been modified` from the in-tree pv-controller losing one optimistic-concurrency race; the manila CSI provisioner bound the volume one minute **after** the runner failed the start — every first start of every sandbox failed), and the recovery buttons do nothing: **Retry now** 409s while the failure-backoff lease is live (up to 10 min after the third failure) and **Restart** answers a bare 202 on a `failed` sandbox (`request_restart` matches `starting`/`running` only; a failed row's `restart_requested_at` stamp is claimed by no arm). Fix: the runner's fail-fast distinguishes a bind failure (`FailedBinding` always fatal) from a slow provisioner (`ProvisioningFailed` fatal only when no newer Normal provisioning event supersedes it and it is older than `PVC_PROVISION_GRACE` 90 s — otherwise keep waiting with `provisioning` progress, readiness deadline still the backstop); the failure message names the claim's actual class and drops the storage-class hint when the claim pins one; an explicit retry overrides the backoff lease (C-610 amended); Restart from `failed` performs the same recovery through the C-610 path (423 `frozen` included, audit `via: "restart"`), and from `stopped`/`unprovisioned`/`stopping` answers 409 `restart_not_applicable` instead of a bare 202 (C-626); the Computer tab's Restart button is disabled on the 409 states and enabled on `failed` (owner report, `docs/tasks/FIX-049.md` normative, D-164, C-610 amended + C-626) | owner report 2026-11-12, D-158/D-160 amendments | S–M | Rust + web | — | runner `backends/kubernetes/mod.rs` (`unbound_home`, `warning_events`, hint) + its k8s_mock tests, `crates/aurora-db/src/repo/sandboxes.rs` (`retry_failed_start`), api `domains/hosted/routes.rs` (restart/retry), `web/src/components/agents/computer-tab.tsx`, contracts C-610/C-626, docs operator/runners.md, fix_029 it-tests | `docs/tasks/FIX-049.md` | moved to phase 4 (2026-11-13: owner production incident, never implemented — the review found the spec only; row FIX-049 in §Phase 4 tasks) |
| FIX-050 | An org created by the OIDC claim sync is born without an owner, and the resulting gateway refusals are invisible (owner incident 2026-11-12: every LLM call from every sandbox failed `ERR_PROXY_TUNNEL` — a 407 at CONNECT auth; root cause: org `users` claim-created 2026-11-10 by a `member`-only mapping, audit `member.add … "created_org": true`; `ensure_org` removes `create_organization`'s bootstrap owner unconditionally; the gateway's credential lookup requires an active org owner, so **every** CONNECT of **every** credential of the org was refused 407 with no log line and no request-log row, while message delivery kept working; `active_owners=0` proven by SQL, repaired by a manual `UPDATE org_members`). Fix: owner-integrity floor — after the sync's org step an org the mappings touched that would end the transaction ownerless gets its syncing user promoted to owner (claim-derived `idp_role` kept, audited `reason: "ownerless_org"`, suspended members never promoted, last-owner guard then protects the floor); `GET /v1/admin/overview` reports `ownerless_orgs` + Admin warning banner; every CONNECT-auth refusal logs `connect_auth_refused` with a reason class (`unknown|revoked|expired|no_active_owner|frozen|store_unavailable`, kind, source address — never token material) and metric `aurora_gateway_connect_refused_total{reason}` (owner report, `docs/tasks/FIX-050.md` normative, D-165, C-630) | owner incident 2026-11-12 (same day as FIX-049, independent) | S–M | Rust + web | — | api `domains/oidc/roles.rs` (`ensure_org`/`sync` floor), `crates/aurora-db/src/repo/members.rs` (owner-count helper), api `domains/connectors` overview DTO, gateway `auth.rs`/`proxy/connect.rs` (refusal logging + metric), web admin banner, contract C-630, docs 04/06, oidc_roles + admin + gateway it-tests | `docs/tasks/FIX-050.md` | done (5af32da7, merged 98c0627b): oidc_roles 22/22, gateway 58+211 (incl. outage refusal-class e2e), web tsc + 541 vitest, gen:api no-diff on the merged tree; clean auto-merge over FIX-051/052/053 + P4-TERM despite the 17-commit-stale base |
| FIX-052 | The gateway sends a `Host` header next to `:authority` on HTTP/2 upstream hops; strict front doors (nginx) answer a bare 400 (owner incident 2026-11-12: after FIX-050's repair, every LLM call failed `400 Bad Request`; `request_log` showed `decision=allow, injected=1, upstream_status=400` and the body was an **nginx HTML page** from `aigw.cern.ch`, not litellm JSON — rejected at the front door; identical rows from 2026-11-10 19:51 prove the endpoint never worked through Aurora, hidden behind the FIX-050 407 wall). Root cause reproduced against the live endpoint with a raw h2 client: `Upstream::send` re-inserts `Host` (`upstream.rs:311-323`) → h2 wire carries `:authority: host:443` **and** `host: host`; RFC 9113 §8.3.1 forbids the pair, nginx rejects it (hyper-based test upstreams accept it, which is why every test passed; python-h2 even refuses the block client-side). Fix: delete the manual insert (hyper-util synthesizes `Host` on h1 hops byte-identically, default ports dropped); refuse a `host`-targeting injection header at snapshot load; pin both protocols with a raw-h2 upstream it-test (owner incident, `docs/tasks/FIX-052.md` normative, D-166, C-633; numbered 052 — the owner's merged test-only runner-streams commit already took 051) | owner incident 2026-11-12 (same chain as FIX-050, next layer) | S | Rust | — | gateway `upstream.rs` (`send` Host insert), `inject.rs`/snapshot load (host-injection refusal), it-tests `proxy.rs` (raw h2 upstream), contract C-633, docs 06 step 13 | `docs/tasks/FIX-052.md` | done (8f8d391a, merged fffb4301): gateway 266 green on the lane, re-verified on the merged tree; raw-h2 upstream test pins no-`Host`-on-h2; systematic front-door oracles → TEST-021 (queued) |

| P5-ICS | Owner 2026-11-14 (phase 5, D-273): read-only calendar access without an OAuth client ("I can't get [one] without full official permission from our microsoft admins"); "a generic ICS calendar app"; "why not just have the user give us a name and the full URL … hardcoding providers is not scalable". A new generic **secret-link** credential kind (C-843, SEC-107): the person enters a name and the full URL (any provider, self-hosted servers under `allowed_private`); the agent requests `https://<connection_id>.link.aurora.internal/` (listed by name in `~/.aurora/links.json`) and the gateway `GET`s exactly the stored URL: destination guard and org egress policy on the real host and every redirect hop, the URL never in the sandbox, logs, traces, approvals or API reads (host only); the gateway caches a link's body for 5 minutes, per connection, after admission (owner). This is the database lane's pattern. Package `ics-calendar`: one `fields` method, one read tool, setup guide per common provider (no code per provider); recurrences expanded by a pinned `icalendar` + `recurring-ical-events` recipe | owner, D-273, C-843, SEC-107, D-80/D-89 pattern | M–L | Rust (registry, gateway, API connect, home sync, supervisor) + package | p5-apps hot files (registry manifest, gateway decision) | `crates/aurora-registry`, gateway `link` module, API connect + home sync `links`, supervisor `links.json`, `limits.rs`, `plugins/builtin/ics-calendar/**`, `docs/06`/`09`/`12`/`17` | `docs/tasks/P5-ICS.md` | ready (rewritten 2026-11-14 to the owner's whole-URL design; the Orchestrator places it, `p5-apps` files) |
| P5-CALINV | Owner 2026-11-14 (phase 5, D-274): agents write to any calendar (Outlook at CERN included, with no OAuth) by **sending the person an invitation**. Agent tools `calendar_invite`/`_update`/`_cancel`/`_list` (C-844): an iMIP `REQUEST`/`CANCEL` from Aurora's mailer (`email.from`) to the turn's person at their verified email, and nobody else (SEC-108). A fixed preface names the agent; the calendar is library-built (escaping, folding; `VTIMEZONE` for repeats); rate-limited; org switch `calendar_invitations.enabled`; a `calendar_invitations` table for update/cancel/list (no description or location kept). Owner-run live check in CERN Outlook and Gmail (accept, update, cancel, a weekly repeat across a DST change) | owner, D-274, C-844, SEC-108 | M | Rust (API tools, mailer, db) | instance mailer configured for the live check | API `domains/tools/calendar.rs` + registration, mailer send function, `aurora-db` migration `20261201000230` + repo, retention/deletion hooks, org setting, docs `10`/`12`/`15`/`17` | `docs/tasks/P5-CALINV.md` | ready (not yet placed; disjoint from the `p5-apps` hot files) |
| P5-CALDAV | Owner 2026-11-14 (phase 5, D-275): read **and** write any CalDAV server (iCloud, Fastmail, Nextcloud, mailbox.org, Posteo, Radicale…) with the person's own app password. New `own_server` manifest section (C-845): the person enters the server URL, connect discovers the calendar home through the destination guard, and the gateway injects `Basic` only on the discovered origins, never on a claimed, instance, LLM or Aurora host (SEC-109; the owner: a person pointing their own password at a server they chose is fine). The agent sees the URL (not secret) but never the password (`~/.aurora/servers.json`). Package `caldav`: tools by WebDAV method, a new `ical` approval decoder listing attendees, scheduling `POST` defaults to Ask; Google CalDAV and Exchange refused with pointers. Radicale container for the ITs | owner, D-275, C-845, SEC-109, D-80/D-89 pattern | L | Rust (registry, gateway, API connect, home sync, supervisor) + package | after P5-ICS in the same lane (registry, gateway decision, home sync) | `crates/aurora-registry` (+ `ical` decoder), gateway own-server injection, API discovery + home sync `servers`, supervisor `servers.json`, `plugins/builtin/caldav/**`, Radicale test container, docs `06`/`09`/`12`/`17` | `docs/tasks/P5-CALDAV.md` | ready (not yet placed; after P5-ICS) |

## Phase 5 at closing (moved from PROGRESS on 2026-11-28)

Phase 5 was closed by the owner on 2026-11-28 ("close out phase 5 so we clean up progress.md"). What follows is moved **verbatim** from `docs/PROGRESS.md` as it stood then (`git show <close commit>^:docs/PROGRESS.md`); only section headings were levelled. Eleven rows were carried to PROGRESS §Phase 6 tasks instead (three running lanes, the rest not started): FIX-284, FIX-290, FIX-292, FIX-285, FIX-293, FIX-294, FIX-295, P5-FOREMAN, P5-RUNDECK, P5-SERVICENOW, P5-ZOOM.


Columns: **Size** S (≤ 1 day of one lane) / M (a few days) / L (a week or more, several crates or both stacks). **Lane** Rust / Next.js / both / docs / ops / package (`plugins/builtin/**` only) / tests. **Refs**: decisions, contracts, SEC invariants and migrations the task owns or implements. **Brief**: `docs/tasks/<file>`, or "needed".

Shared files that no lane owns (each lane appends or regenerates them, and the coordinator re-generates at merge): `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (one new section per lane), `web/openapi.json` + `web/src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` (regenerated), `crates/aurora-db/tests/fixtures/spec_schema.json` and the machine-checked tables of `docs/03` (each migration lane moves its own `§Planned changes` rows), `docs/tests/security-coverage.md` (append rows), `services/aurora-api/tests/it/permission_matrix.rs` (append rows), `Cargo.lock` (only lanes adding a dependency; serialize those merges).

#### Phase 5 tasks

Lane order, waves and number blocks: `docs/tasks/PHASE5-PLAN.md`. Lanes `p5-apps`, `p5-agent`, `p5-platform`. Scope, ownership and acceptance live in each brief; the full status notes written before 2026-11-15 are kept in `docs/history/phase-5.md`. Status values: `done (merged <sha>)` · `in-progress (lane <lane>; committed <sha> on alexiri/<lane>, not merged)` · `in-progress (lane <lane>; started)` · `ready` (brief written; "queued in lane X" when it is in a running lane's chain) · `not started` (no brief yet) · `deferred` / `moved` / `cancelled`.

Fix: `replay_body` keeps the framing (empty → no body, ended → exact `Content-Length`); the wire IT failed before (6dced814); live geocode/get_place/get_directions 200 through a local gateway with the fix, 500 without. Gate: gateway 446/446, clippy, guards, gateway.sh. Not run: kind k7 (compiles; no throwaway cluster); the hold/park and LLM paths are unit-tested through `replay_body`, no new end-to-end IT. | FIX-234 | **Owner 2026-11-23, security fix.** Org owners/admins were implicit owners of every workspace: they could read anyone's direct chats, chat with anyone's agent (which then used that person's accounts, e.g. sent mail as them), approve its requests and grant its accounts. Gate on the final tree: fmt, workspace clippy, aurora-db 58/58, the whole aurora-api suite 1900/1900, schema, guards; the supervisor re-ran whole-project web lint, the whole vitest suite (265 files, 3679 tests) and the build on the merged tree. OPEN for the owner: SCIM and IdP-driven membership into groups bound to SHARED workspaces is treated as trusted (an administrator who can mint SCIM tokens can add people there without the session/notice rules); whether instance admins need their own audit (the review could not audit all 35 call sites)) |

#### Execution order (phase 5)

- **P5-NAV-1..5 (owner 2026-11-27: "write briefs for nav-2a and nav-3, plus their reviews. All your proposed decisions are accepted. Send the briefs to Orchestrator for implementation").** The Navigation review; every brief reads its target from `docs/design/navigation/` (D-507, D-508). Web only, no API change. Suggested lanes: **NAV-1** (agent pages) in one lane; **NAV-2 → NAV-3** (places, then the picker; both change `app-shell.tsx`) in another. **NAV-4** reviews NAV-1 when it merges; **NAV-5** reviews NAV-2 and NAV-3 when both have merged; both loop send-backs through the Orchestrator until clean.

- **P5-MEMORY-1..5 and P5-SKILLS-1..5 (owner 2026-11-27: "Write briefs for this next UX polish round with all your changes. Make sure the mockups are visible to the agents this time … Add a brief at the end of each part (memory and skills) to compare the work done with the original mockups and order more work if needed. Once the briefs are done, have orchestrator queue them up").** The Skills & Memory review; every brief reads its target from `docs/design/skills-memory/` (in the repository, D-496). Suggested lanes (at most three at a time; the Orchestrator places them):
  - **Lane 1 (Rust):** P5-MEMORY-1 → (web) P5-MEMORY-4.
  - **Lane 2 (Rust):** P5-SKILLS-1 → (web) P5-SKILLS-4 (after P5-SKILLS-3).
  - **Lane 3 (web):** P5-SKILLS-2 (no dependency, starts at once) → P5-MEMORY-2 → P5-MEMORY-3 → P5-SKILLS-3 (needs P5-SKILLS-1 and the shared editor from P5-MEMORY-2).
  - Security reviews before merge: P5-MEMORY-1 (SEC-157), P5-SKILLS-1 (SEC-158). Migrations `000288` (MEMORY-1), `000289`, `000290` (SKILLS-1); renumber at merge if a guard needs ascending order.
  - **Last:** P5-MEMORY-5 after MEMORY-1..4, P5-SKILLS-5 after SKILLS-1..4, each in a fresh review lane with a throwaway cluster; they loop send-backs through the Orchestrator until a pass is clean.

- **P5-REFINE-1..20 (owner 2026-11-20: "create briefs to implement all these changes, have the Orchestrator start implementing them in parallel and add a Brief at the end to verify … if they don't, they need to be sent back and iterated on until they do").** UX review 5 with Before/After mockups for every finding and the owner's comments applied (<https://claude.ai/artifact/1YJcRA7Q84864pkim44Qnt>, D-405; read-only copy `/home/alex/orca/workspaces/aurora/design-ux-review-5/`). Six lanes (the owner's maximum), heavy commands through `heavy.sh`; a brief starts when its "Depends on" briefs have merged, and a web brief whose later items wait for a backend brief does its other items first:
  - **Lane A (web):** P5-REFINE-1 → P5-REFINE-11 → P5-REFINE-15 → P5-REFINE-19.
  - **Lane B (web):** P5-REFINE-2 → P5-REFINE-13 → P5-REFINE-14.
  - **Lane C (small Rust, then web):** P5-REFINE-3 → P5-REFINE-12 → P5-REFINE-16.
  - **Lane D (web):** P5-REFINE-17 → P5-REFINE-18 (starts when 1, 2 and 3 have merged).
  - **Lane E (Rust):** P5-REFINE-4 → P5-REFINE-7 → P5-REFINE-9.
  - **Lane F (Rust):** P5-REFINE-5 → P5-REFINE-6 → P5-REFINE-8 → P5-REFINE-10.
  - **Start now, in parallel:** P5-REFINE-1, -2, -3, -4, -5. Where 1 (strings) and 2 (primitives) touch the same component, 1 merges first and 2 rebases.
  - **Last:** P5-REFINE-20 when 1–19 have merged: it verifies every item against its mockup and sends each difference back to the owning lane; the lanes stay open for its send-backs until a pass ends with nothing to change.
  - **Since 2026-11-20 night: three lanes (owner).** Merged: 1–8, 10, 13, 15, 17 and the main passes of 11, 12, 16, 18; P5-SCHED-ONCE A+B. Workers own every test their change affects; the Orchestrator reviews each report against the brief, the mockups and the tests and sends back anything not complete.
    - **Lane A (`p5-polish-a`, web):** P5-REFINE-19 (merged 2026-11-21) → P5-REFINE-20 send-back batch 1, items 1–8 (merged 993ffaa2) → batch 2, items 1–10 (merged 2026-11-21) → batch 3: the key-verification hang, Computer, Administration (merged 2026-11-21: the hang came from an endpoint the API cannot reach; #### Exit criteria (phase 5)

In the spirit of `docs/audit/phase0-exit.md`: one commit, a fresh `target/`, every command's exit code and result recorded, defects fixed as separate tasks and re-run. The phase-5 exit audit's scope is not defined yet; at least the items 4–11 of the earlier audits (`docs/history/phase-1-2.md` §Phase exit criteria) on the phase-5 commit, and every SEC invariant added for phase 5 (SEC-86 and SEC-88 onward, for what was built) proven. There is no multi-arch criterion: OPS-022 is cancelled (D-318).

## Features (rows moved from FEATURES.md at closing, 2026-11-28)

Every `done` or `cancelled` row that was not phase 6's, moved verbatim (statuses reconciled with git history in the same commit, before the move).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RFE-011b§park | Approval park-and-resume: a long-deadline approval parks (the request is refused `approval_parked` at once, the turn ends, the sandbox may sleep), a person decides later, approval writes a single-use grant and a follow-up that resumes the agent, the identical request goes through exactly once; LLM traffic never parks; parking is an instance-wide switch (`80 §RFE-011` Decision; `07 §Park and resume`; P4-011B spec batch 69d51553, D-264, D-265, D-269(a)(b); SEC-74) | P5 (moved, D-138) | P4-011B-1, P4-011B-2, P4-011B-5 (e2e) | P4-011B-4 | SPEC-P4 | done (P4-011B-1..5 merged; last 7db66590) | — |
| RFE-011b§webpush | Web Push for approvals: an opted-in decider's browser is notified when an approval waits and no Aurora tab is open — instance VAPID key, per-browser subscriptions bound to the session, encrypted fixed-field pushes to allow-listed push services, a service worker that only shows fixed text and opens Aurora; `push.*` stays in Helm (`80 §RFE-011`; `07 §Web Push`; `14`; D-266, D-267, D-269(c)(d); SEC-75, SEC-76, SEC-102) | P5 (moved, D-138) | P4-011B-3, P4-011B-5 (e2e) | P4-011B-4 | SPEC-P4 | done (P4-011B-1..5 merged; last 7db66590) | — |
